                                     β    q

where pβ < ∞ is fixed for each |β| ≤ 2sG . Here Dpiv denotes differentiation in any fixed left-invariant
frame of a compact group variable occurring in the core. It is independent of the three structural/exterior

For a fixed Cauchy polydisc and b1 > b0 , choose ε > 0 with (1 + ε)b0 < b1 . Then

                                  b0 qRX (η + Re h) ≤ b1 qRX (η) + Cε,δ |X|.

Cauchy’s formula on the fixed domain therefore gives the differentiated total-variation rows without using
an equal-weight growth improvement.
   For collar and outer labels, differentiate under the real finite-dimensional integral. Lemma 3.5 shows
that every derivative of a splice cutoff remains supported on the charged annulus or outer event. All
remaining terms are finite Bell polynomials in fixed-template derivatives. By Lemma 3.1, the same
statement holds after any |β| ≤ 2sG compact-variable derivatives. Such derivatives add only a fixed
polynomial in u−1 , χ−1 , and |X|; they do not alter the charged support. These polynomial losses multiply
the same action exponential and are absorbed after decreasing its constant c.
   Lemma 3.19 divides out the reference denominator. Its ud0 |X|/2 , Rg2 |X|, and finite chart losses are
absorbed by the assumed action dominance after decreasing c. The finite label sum changes only C.
   The reference density has the same fixed analytic domain without a bad cutoff. Cauchy and the
trial lower bound control its normalized derivatives. Bell-polynomial differentiation of G−1  X preserves
only per-core polynomial/exponential losses, which are absorbed when multiplied by the bad numerator
estimate.

                                                     18
3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.
Lemma 3.25 (Exact connected coarea allocation). On the permanent weak tube the field-dependent
coarea logarithm has an absolutely convergent connected expansion
                                           X               X
                    − log det(I + DN P ) =     dA ,    sup     eacoa |A| ∥dA ∥[3] < ∞.
                                                             e
                                               A                 A∋e

For every compatible X the owner map of Definition 3.22 gives the exact identity
                                 X                X
                                   dA = Dreg +        DX + Dcross ,
                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.


                                                      19
Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
convergent word expansion. Report every word on the union of its canonical connector paths. The
permanent support estimate pays all convolutions. Every reported word has one connected support, so
the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.
Lemma 3.26 (Marked Gaussian block-polymer estimate). Let {HX }X∈X be finite-dimensional core
spaces and let
                                                    −1/2        −1/2
                           M

                                               X                    0≤j≤q
                                                   Y ̸=X

Assume ∥K∥aG ,[3] ≤ κ < 1/8, the exterior means and their first three marks are uniformly bounded in the
  1/2
QXX metric, and each normalized one-core non-Gaussian factor has uniformly bounded logarithmic scores
through order three. Then the exact density ratio between the joint Gaussian with precision Qdiag + Qcross
and the product of the one-core Gaussians, including the shifted means and scalar normalization, has a
connected core-polymer logarithm
                                           dγX           X
                                     log N          =             GA
                                            X dγX             A⊂X mconn

with                                       X                               κ
                                  sup          eaG |A| ∥GA ∥[3] ≤ CG             .                        (2)

                                        A∋X
The same row holds after replacing each dγX by its normalized one-core reference. The constant is uniform
under the standing convention and in the number and sizes of cores.
                                                             1/2
Proof. Center and normalize each block by yX = QXX (zX − mX ). The determinant part is
                                 1                 1 X (−1)n+1
                                   Tr log(I + K) =             Tr K n .
                                 2                 2      n
                                                             n≥1

Every trace word is a cyclic connected word in the core-incidence graph. Rooting it at one visited core
and using the weighted block row gives, after q ≤ 3 marks,
                                   X                         κ
                                       nq (4κ)n /n ≤ Cq             .

Here the factor four pays orientation, the choice of the marked edge, and the conversion from cyclic to

                                                                                               1/2
   For the quadratic and exterior-shift part interpolate the off-diagonal precision as Q(t) = Qdiag (I +
        1/2
tK)Qdiag , 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, and apply the finite-dimensional BKAR formula to the block parameters. A
differentiated edge inserts either ⟨yX , KXY yY ⟩ or one of its marked mean/precision variants. No positivity
of KXY is used: the absolute-value allocation
                                                    1
                               |⟨yX , KXY yY ⟩| ≤     ∥KXY ∥(∥yX ∥2 + ∥yY ∥2 )
                                                    2
assigns a nonnegative quadratic regulator to the two incident vertices. The weighted block row makes
the sum of those regulator matrices at every replica vertex at most Cκ. After enlarging RG so 4Cκ < 1,
Corollary 4.2 bounds all Gaussian exponential moments uniformly in the replica matrix and in the number
of cores. The geometric reporting lemma assigns each differentiated tree to its connected core hull. The
exact 1/n! and Lemma 4.3 absorb the vertex factorials. The resulting rooted tree series has link mass at
most 4κ and gives the same denominator as the determinant words.
   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

   For collar and outer labels, differentiate under the real finite-dimensional integral. Lemma 3.5 shows
that every derivative of a splice cutoff remains supported on the charged annulus or outer event. All
remaining terms are finite Bell polynomials in fixed-template derivatives. By Lemma 3.1, the same
statement holds after any |β| ≤ 2sG compact-variable derivatives. Such derivatives add only a fixed
polynomial in u−1 , χ−1 , and |X|; they do not alter the charged support. These polynomial losses multiply
the same action exponential and are absorbed after decreasing its constant c.
   Lemma 3.19 divides out the reference denominator. Its ud0 |X|/2 , Rg2 |X|, and finite chart losses are
absorbed by the assumed action dominance after decreasing c. The finite label sum changes only C.
   The reference density has the same fixed analytic domain without a bad cutoff. Cauchy and the
trial lower bound control its normalized derivatives. Bell-polynomial differentiation of G−1  X preserves
only per-core polynomial/exponential losses, which are absorbed when multiplied by the bad numerator
estimate.

Then, in the weakened exterior regulator,

Proof. Differentiate by Leibniz. Bound every differentiated density by the theorem and every differentiated
insertion by its declared norm. Derivatives of G−1 X are finite Bell polynomials in normalized reference
scores.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

                                 X                X

                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.

Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely

the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

                               

                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.

                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)

                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.

excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by

factor occurs.

4      Permanent weak renormalization group
Let H be a finite-dimensional real Hilbert space, let C ⪰ 0 be a covariance on H, and let S = (sij )ni,j=1 ⪰ 0
have sii = 1. Put Σ = S ⊗ C.
  For each i, let Bi ⪰ 0 and put B = diag(B1 , . . . , Bn ).

                                                             n

   For the quadratic and exterior-shift part interpolate the off-diagonal precision as Q(t) = Qdiag (I +
        1/2

Corollary 4.2 bounds all Gaussian exponential moments uniformly in the replica matrix and in the number
of cores. The geometric reporting lemma assigns each differentiated tree to its connected core hull. The
exact 1/n! and Lemma 4.3 absorb the vertex factorials. The resulting rooted tree series has link mass at
most 4κ and gives the same denominator as the determinant words.
   A structural or source derivative either differentiates one block of K, one exterior mean, or one one-core
score. Leibniz distributes at most three marks, producing the fourth power in (2). Finally, replacing dγX
by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

                                                     21
Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.
  Let ζY (u, R) be the complete regular insertion family and define

    η0 (u, R) = sup     ea|Y | ∥ζY (u, R)∥[3] , η1 (u, R) = sup sup   ea|Y | ∥DR ζY (u, R)[H]∥[3] .

Differentiating under the integral through order three expresses every mark as a finite sum of resolvent
words. The lower bound gives ∥(Q + t)−1 ∥ ≤ (2 + t)−1 ; convolution of the printed exponential precision

rows therefore yields the same rooted row for Q−1/2 and for K = Qdiag Qcross Qdiag . This proves (i).
  The exterior linear term ℓX (η) is a fixed-incidence contraction of the exponentially local precision with

the exterior regulator row give ∥QXX mX ∥[3] ≤ C, proving (ii).

                                  D log pX = D log ρX − EpX [D log ρX ],

and higher derivatives are finite cumulant polynomials of the corresponding unnormalized scores. The
exterior-tube bounds, the displayed collar/outer TV rows, and the plateau lower bound for GX bound
these cumulants through order three without a volume factor. This proves (iii) in the declared rooted
marked norm.

                                                     21
Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.
  Let ζY (u, R) be the complete regular insertion family and define
                    X                                               X
    η0 (u, R) = sup     ea|Y | ∥ζY (u, R)∥[3] , η1 (u, R) = sup sup   ea|Y | ∥DR ζY (u, R)[H]∥[3] .
                  e                                                   ∥H∥[3] ≤1    e
                      Y ∋e                                                             Y ∋e

The regular supplier gives

                              η0 (u, R) ≤ C(uω + ∥R∥[3] ),                 η1 (u, R) ≤ C.

For a connected bad core let
                           h            2
                                            i|X|/νov
                  bu (X) = Cb u−p e−cb χ /u          ,                ∥TX F ∥[3] ≤ C0 bu (X)∥F ∥[3] .

                                |X |
core support reserve absorbs C0 . For a bad parent X, the total inherited-child mass is at most C|X|η0 ;
for an inherited parent Y , the total bad-child mass is at most C|Y |θu . The size factors are absorbed by
cP ab < ac < a.
   Dominate each connected bipartite incidence graph by a rooted spanning tree rooted at a bad core.
The zero-order marked product estimate has a cubic vertex polynomial and gives (1 − m∗ )−4 . A Frechet
derivative marks one inherited vertex; together with three structural marks this gives the first four
derivatives of the geometric recursion and hence (1 − m∗ )−5 . Lemma 3.29 ensures that this tree sum is
over a partition of the exact histories, not a duplicated majorant. Every sum is rooted, so no volume
factor occurs.

                                     ≤N

for every auxiliary replica family, and that the parent-normalized rooted tree link constant, after the fixed
exponential losses and the factor σ −2 , is strictly below one.
   Then the normalized connected logarithm, summed with its exact 1/n!, converges absolutely in a
weakened rooted support norm and is Fréchet-C N in the declared structural marks, uniformly in volume,
replica number, forest parameters, and previous RG depth.
Proof. Differentiate the BKAR functional along a tree. A hard-core derivative costs one overlap bond
and no field derivative. A covariance derivative costs one covariance connector and one field derivative at
each endpoint. If di covariance edges meet vertex i, the analytic row contributes di !σ −di Aαi ,N .
   The Gram lemma bounds every auxiliary Gaussian regulator moment uniformly in the replica family.
Summing the choices of hard-core versus covariance derivatives costs only 2n−1 . The Pruefer lemma
shows that the exact 1/n! in the connected logarithm absorbs the product of vertex factorials with only
an exponential loss. The remaining rooted tree sum is the ordinary parent-normalized geometric recursion
in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N .

4.3    Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

insertion, the one-loop identity L = I − V + 1 = 1 shows that every additional quantum vertex is
a tree attachment to the unique loop; after those tree attachments are collected, the insertion enters
the background two-point amplitude through one effective two-quantum-leg kernel. Two fluctuation
derivatives choose an ordered pair of edges in X, producing at most C|X|2 terms. Rooted parallel
transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the

operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Lemma 5.15 (Scale-independent finite scheme map). Let gW be the local standard coupling on the
canonically rescaled pure Wilson family and let g⋆ be the same local functional on the full centered
invariant family. On the common weak interval there is one C 2 map
                                  s(g) = g + cs g 2 + O(g 3 ),      s′ (0) = 1,
independent of the dyadic lattice spacing, such that
                                                    g⋆ = s(gW )
at both ends of every factor-two step. Consequently
                                            Σstd = s ◦ ΣW ◦ s−1 .
The C 3 -flat irregular complement is included in s; no analyticity at g = 0 is claimed or needed.
Proof. Evaluate the same canonically rescaled nonexceptional projector P⋆ on the two full weak families.
Their tree Hessians coincide. Their first difference is the invariant-graph tangent uH1 , whose vacuum
scalar and complete dimension-four retained projection vanish. Lemma 5.14 proves, rather than assumes,
that the entire exponentially quasi-local insertion family contributes a finite scale-independent transverse
scheme row and no new unsuppressed log a coefficient. SI–6 excludes an independent background
dimension-four counterterm. The inner-chart difference is therefore a finite C 2 unit-tangent redefinition.
U4 adds a C 3 -flat term through the two consumed background marks, which changes neither s′ (0) nor its
quadratic coefficient. Canonical rescaling identifies the profile, gauge fermion, subtraction projector, and
cutoff with the same unit-lattice template at every dyadic scale, so the same map s occurs before and
after the step. The displayed conjugacy follows from the definitions.

N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .

  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is

                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff

   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).
Corollary 6.40 (Exact post-compact joint partition). Fix the compatible optical-core family after the
finite RG merge. There are normalized one-core references dµref
                                                              X,J and one exact cross-core activity
inventory
                                AJ = AJG ⊔ AJreg ⊔ AJirr ⊔ AJH
such that                                   Z O
                                Z(J)                                X       Y
                                        =           X,J (ζX )
                                                  dµref                             zA,J (ζA ).          (100)
                               Zloc (J)
                                              X                 F compatible A∈F

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

                                                         1                           1

                          u∈I⋆                           2                           4

Then the complete joint branch mass is at most 3δB /4, strictly inside the same admission ball.
Proof. The regular supplier gives η0 ≤ C(uω + ∥R⋆ (u)∥[3] ), the bad-core mass is exponentially small, and
both κG (RG ) and ηH (RG ) tend to zero as RG → ∞. Choose the parameters in the displayed order. This
is the same strict inequality consumed by the existing irregular and Gaussian forest theorems.
Lemma 6.42 (Cycle-safe compact spatial row). The complete compact activity estimate is exactly (99).
In particular every discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch is weighted before summation,
and ηH (RG ) ≤ Ce−mH RG for one mH > 0.
Proof. This is the final paragraph of Section 6.5.4; the residual edge-weight reserve pays root and anchor
multiplicity.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).
Corollary 6.40 (Exact post-compact joint partition). Fix the compatible optical-core family after the
finite RG merge. There are normalized one-core references dµref
                                                              X,J and one exact cross-core activity
inventory
                                AJ = AJG ⊔ AJreg ⊔ AJirr ⊔ AJH
such that                                   Z O

                                        =           X,J (ζX )
                                                  dµref                             zA,J (ζA ).          (100)
                               Zloc (J)
                                              X                 F compatible A∈F

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Then the complete joint branch mass is at most 3δB /4, strictly inside the same admission ball.
Proof. The regular supplier gives η0 ≤ C(uω + ∥R⋆ (u)∥[3] ), the bad-core mass is exponentially small, and
both κG (RG ) and ηH (RG ) tend to zero as RG → ∞. Choose the parameters in the displayed order. This
is the same strict inequality consumed by the existing irregular and Gaussian forest theorems.
Lemma 6.42 (Cycle-safe compact spatial row). The complete compact activity estimate is exactly (99).

and ηH (RG ) ≤ Ce−mH RG for one mH > 0.

multiplicity.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .
Theorem 6.44 (Two composite-root extension). Fix two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B. For any
prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale
roots, or normalized local roots measured in the permanent finite source-renormalization norm. Then
                                        (Q )   (Q )
                                  K(RA A , RB B ) ≤ CQA ,QB e−mE dist(A,B) ,                                (105)

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.


                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert
those two coefficients as the two distinguished roots of the exact joint compact–optical logarithm; do
not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.


                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert
those two coefficients as the two distinguished roots of the exact joint compact–optical logarithm; do
not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .
Theorem 6.44 (Two composite-root extension). Fix two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B. For any
prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale
roots, or normalized local roots measured in the permanent finite source-renormalization norm. Then
                                        (Q )   (Q )
                                  K(RA A , RB B ) ≤ CQA ,QB e−mE dist(A,B) ,                                (105)

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.


                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert
those two coefficients as the two distinguished roots of the exact joint compact–optical logarithm; do
not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the
exact regular/irregular owner decomposition. Integrating the finite interpolation gives the same estimate.
No claim for arbitrary uncontrolled boundary measures is needed downstream.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated

observable and let B be any bounded registered exterior perturbation supported at distance at least r from
F . Through source order two,

                             DJq ⟨F ; B⟩c J=0 ≤ CF,B,q e−mE r ,     q = 0, 1, 2.                      (107)

The same estimate holds for a finite family of compact-boundary or optical chart perturbations whose
centered score rows are uniformly bounded in the permanent marked norm. This is the boundary-to-center
statement consumed by the source forest.
Proof. Use F and B as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The owner inventory is exhaustive at the
vertex level, and Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported acoustic class. For a finite interpolation
of compact boundary data, differentiation along a unit-speed group geodesic inserts its centered local
score as the exterior root; for an admitted optical chart interpolation the affine derivative inserts the
corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the
exact regular/irregular owner decomposition. Integrating the finite interpolation gives the same estimate.
No claim for arbitrary uncontrolled boundary measures is needed downstream.

row and column sums bounded by Aq through source order two, uniformly in volume, cutoff, exterior
configuration, and preceding weak depth. Use the two buffers as the declared roots in Theorem 6.43; the
vertex inventory is exact by Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8, while Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional
unreported acoustic class.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact

is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner

each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale

                                        (Q )   (Q )
                                  K(RA A , RB B ) ≤ CQA ,QB e−mE dist(A,B) ,                                (105)

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.

Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert

not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).
